 the first guard. It was to ask a question where other people could hear the answer. She had prepared a short version and a second, shorter version in case somebody pulled the microphone away. \"A tiresome habit of theirs,\" she said over the line. \"I have adjusted my technique accordingly.\"","The opening question concerned an apparently simple claim. If verification was voluntary, could a detained subject refuse further contact with a paired node and leave? Selene answered that all lawful safeguards remained in place. It sounded reassuring. It did not answer whether the person could leave. June asked again. The official smile on the large screen became a little less amiable. A second reporter, who had been planning a question about investment growth, put down his notes and listened.","In the upper gallery, Mara watched the remote-start entries remain enabled. \"We need this record somewhere else.\" Cassian took another photograph and wrote the visible time beside the copied identifiers. Ivo could not send the images through their observation line. He could preserve the small local record in the instrument case and send the spoken information separately. The physical material would have to leave with a person. A clever network did not abolish the need for legs.","A warning flashed on the gallery panel. The maintenance desk had flagged an unplanned presence in the old hub. Their entry had not become invisible merely because it was inconvenient to detect. Anika checked the route back and heard footsteps at the stair. The real worker moved to the door. \"I can slow them down,\" he said. Mara looked at his hands, which were already shaking. \"You can tell them we came through here. Then get out with your crew. You have done enough.\"","He hesitated, then nodded. Their collaboration did not require him to defend the gallery with his body. He had an ordinary life beyond the room, and the alliance needed people alive in those lives. Anika led him to the lower passage. Mara, Cassian, and Ivo remained beside the console. The most direct route to the main relay tier was across a short bridge within the tower's outer shell. The door to it was closed, but the maintenance plans still matched the mechanism.","Ivo opened the bridge access using the equipment assigned to his actual trade. Mara did not pretend to understand the instrument in his hand; she kept watch on the stair and held the light where he asked. It was a remarkably effective division of effort. The lock released, and wind swept into the gallery with the cold force of the open tower. Beyond the door, a narrow metal bridge crossed a deep shaft full of cables and structural supports.","Cassian went first. His bruised hand stayed off the rail when it could, accepting help from the other instead of proving anything to an empty shaft. Mara followed with the instrument case. Below, the public hall's light moved across the glass. She could hear applause rise and break up into voices. June had apparently asked the shorter question. \"I assume she is enjoying herself,\" Mara whispered. \"I doubt the same is true of her host,\" Cassian answered.","Halfway across, the lights changed. A motor began to sound in the bridge housing. Ivo looked back at the control panel. The bridge had a movable maintenance section; somebody in the main control room was trying to retract it. \"Forward,\" he said. There was no need to raise his voice. The word carried enough. Cassian reached the far landing and caught Mara's arm as the section beneath her foot shuddered.","She moved, set the case beyond the threshold, and turned. Ivo was still on the center section, one hand locked around a support. The bridge had stopped moving cleanly. An old obstruction beneath it had jammed the mechanism, leaving a gap behind him and a narrowing foothold ahead. The company had attempted to make its hallway into a barrier without knowing who stood on it. A second command could turn the obstruction into a sudden failure.","Mara lay flat on the landing, reached out, and caught his wrist. Cassian braced behind her, keeping his good hand on the doorway rail. Ivo moved one foot toward the remaining edge and swore when it slipped. The case was safe. The documents were safe. None of that mattered more than the terrified man whose fingers were beginning to lose their grip. \"Look at me,\" Mara said. \"Not down. We are moving together.\"","He looked at her. She could see the old fear of water and the new fear of an empty shaft combining in his eyes. A joke would not carry his weight. Cassian shifted, took hold above Ivo's elbow, and bore part of the pull despite the pain in his wrist. Ivo found the final edge, pushed, and came over the threshold in an untidy collision of coats and limbs. Mara rolled clear as the bridge section dropped a few inches and stopped with a metallic crash.","For several seconds, nobody spoke. Then Ivo said, \"I should like to register a complaint about the interactivity.\" Mara began to laugh. Cassian was already laughing, though he had gone pale around the mouth. She caught his hand and checked it before the relief could carry her past the injury. He let her. \"I have kept the same number of fingers,\" he said. \"Your standards remain demanding.\"","Anika's voice came through the receiver. She had reached a lower control point and stopped the bridge motor. The crew was out; no civilian had been left on the moving section. The main room was still locked against them, and guards were coming up through the other stair. They had gained the landing, a record of the enabled nodes, and a new reason not to trust the company's claim that every safety system was under calm supervision.","June reported that the reception had degenerated into an argument no amount of jazz could drown. A staff member had confirmed that several subjects were not free to leave. The clarification was making its way from one reporter to another. It would not bring down the company that night. It would make the next statement harder to issue without scrutiny. Mara pressed the receiver to her ear and thanked her. Then she looked at the door to the relay tier.","Cassian rose beside her. \"We should move before somebody invents a more offensive hallway.\" Ivo collected his instrument case and eyed the door. \"I have always preferred conventional architecture.\" \"You said you adored the old hub.\" \"It was not trying to fold me into a shaft at the time.\" Mara took the plan, found their position, and put her hand on the next latch. Their route had grown shorter. So had their patience."],"questions":[{"type":"Cause / practical limitation","stem":"Why must a person carry the photographed and copied material out of the gallery?","options":["The team has confirmed that no copies exist outside the company.","The available independent link can carry small voice exchanges but not the whole image archive.","Written records are inherently more truthful than every electronic record.","The old observation link can be used only by a company executive."],"answer":1,"explanation":"约束来自链路容量与可传信息种类；因此图像需保存后由人带出，语音信息另传。作者没有宣称纸面天然高于电子记录，也未说外部没有副本。","evidence":[6,10],"shuffled":true}],"wordCount":1692,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0082\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">7</div><h1>10. data-words=\"6015\">patience</a>.</p><section class=\"exercise\"><h2>Reading Practice</h2><p class=\"meta\">先完成选择，再查看解析。练习内容不计入词汇正文覆盖。</p><p class=\"meta\" data-score=\"81\"></p><button type=\"button\" data-reset=\"81\">清空本章作答，重新自测</button><div class=\"question\" id=\"q-82-1\" data-question=\"81,0\"><p class=\"meta\">1. Cause / practical limitation</p><p><strong>Why must a person carry the photographed and copied material out of the gallery?</strong></p><div class=\"options\"><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"0\">A. The team has confirmed that no copies exist outside the company.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"1\">B. The available independent link can carry small voice exchanges but not the whole image archive.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"2\">C. Written records are inherently more truthful than every electronic record.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"3\">D. The old observation link can be used only by a company executive.</button></div><div class=\"feedback\" hidden=\"hidden\"></div></div></section><nav class=\"chapter-nav\" aria-label=\"Chapter navigation\"><button data-go=\"80\">← Previous</button><button data-go=\"82\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"v07c11","title":"11. The Room That Would Not Obey","volume":"7","anchor":"chapter-0083","paragraphs":["The door to the upper relay room opened three inches and stopped against a security bar. Mara looked through the gap at a man in a dark uniform. He looked back at her, at Cassian, and at Ivo holding a case that was plainly not a bouquet. \"You are not the scheduled crew,\" he said. Mara glanced at the jammed bridge behind her. \"Our welcome has given us a strong awareness of that.\"","The man raised his radio. Cassian spoke before he could use it. \"There are workers on the levels below. Whoever ordered that bridge to retract did not clear the area first.\" The guard's eyes moved past them to the displaced section. He could see the damage. His hand did not lower. \"I have instructions.\" \"Then ask who will be accountable for the people caught between them.\"","The exchange bought seconds, not agreement. Mara used them to examine the doorframe. A service opening stood beside it, part of the older ventilation enclosure. The grille had been removed during the maintenance job and set against the wall. Its narrow gallery ran outside the room, behind the tower's outer shell. She looked toward the upper edge and then toward Ivo. He followed her glance, checked the drawing, and nodded once.","Cassian saw the direction of her attention. \"That route is outside.\" \"Only a little.\" \"A highly reassuring measurement.\" She touched his cheek with the back of her hand. \"Keep him talking. You have had a great deal of practice.\" His eyes held hers for a beat. Then he stepped toward the gap in the door, careful to stay visible, and began asking the guard to bring the responsible supervisor to the landing.","Mara climbed into the service opening. The metal was cold beneath her palms. A gust caught her coat as she reached the outer gallery, lifting its hem against the rail. Below, Asterfall spread in broken bands of light: the port, the residential streets, the old theater district. Somewhere in that maze, her mother sat in a hospital room with a nurse nearby. Somewhere else, people still waited beside paired stations they could not freely leave.","She fastened the safety line to the route Ivo had checked and moved along the narrow edge. It was not a comfortable place for a person who preferred solid floors. It was an appropriate place for someone who understood how to keep three points of contact and did not mistake haste for courage. Her boot slipped on a wet patch. She stopped, let the line take part of her weight, found the edge again, and continued. The city did not need her to make the movement prettier than survival allowed.","The gallery reached a second maintenance hatch above the relay room. Through its small pane, she could see two operators and a security supervisor beside the main local console. One operator had a hand pressed against his forehead. The supervisor was ordering him to close a route that the screen marked occupied. The operator refused to confirm it had been cleared. Their conflict had begun before Mara arrived, and it offered her more than any elaborate deception she could invent.","She opened the hatch and lowered herself onto the top of a storage cabinet. The descent made a noise she would have disliked in any other circumstances. Everyone looked up. \"Good evening,\" she said, stepping down beside the operator. \"I understand you have a maintenance problem.\" The supervisor reached for her. She pulled the empty chair in front of his knees and moved to the other side of the desk. He struck the chair, recovered, and came after her.","Mara ducked beneath his first reach, caught the edge of the desk, and swung herself across the narrow gap beside it. She was faster than he was, but he was stronger and the room was small. His hand closed around her upper arm. She twisted, using the movement he had given her, and drove the chair between them again. The operator slapped the door release. The security bar at the landing clicked open. Cassian entered before the supervisor could decide which danger deserved his attention first.","The guard at the door followed, uncertain. Cassian put himself between Mara and the supervisor, but did not strike until the man raised a baton toward her. He caught the arm with both hands, turned the blow aside, and forced it down against the desk. Pain flashed across his face; the bruised wrist had not enjoyed the work. Mara took the baton from the supervisor's loosened grip and slid it beyond reach. Ivo shut the door behind them. The room went suddenly quiet except for the fans.","The operator turned his screen toward the guard. \"That bridge was occupied,\" he said. \"I told him.\" The guard looked at the warning record, then at the security supervisor. His indecision had found a specific fact to stand on. He helped secure the man's hands and called for medical assistance rather than another assault team. It was not a declaration that he had joined Mara's cause. It was a decision not to cooperate with the next order merely because the previous one had been loud.","Anika reached the room through the internal route after the lower crew opened it. She and Ivo checked the local console together. Their workload had become smaller in one sense and more serious in another: they could supervise this relay tier, not the whole tower. The main command links still passed through a separate secured room below. Other city services used adjoining routes. A reckless shutdown could interfere with work they meant to protect. They would establish a controlled local position before changing anything else.","Mara let them work. Her arm hurt. Cassian's hand was swelling again. The moment she noticed it, she took his glove off and set a folded cloth around the wrist. \"You were supposed to keep him talking,\" she murmured. \"He had ceased to be conversational.\" She looked up. A smile threatened the edge of his exhaustion. She kissed him once, short and unsteady, then stayed close enough that he did not have to ask her to remain.","The operator's name was Sol. He was no spymaster, and had no ambition to become a strategist. He had spent fourteen years making sure messages went where their senders intended. The current administration had taught him how dangerous that apparently modest capability could become. He helped Ivo distinguish the old observation route from the new scheduling channel. On the screen, a small green line appeared. The independent voice connection was stable. It was one achievement they could actually name.","June's voice reached the room. She and the outside witnesses were clear of the reception hall. The longer recording had gone to two printers and several reporters; the latest official attempt to dismiss it as an unverified rumor was already being questioned. Leah had the crew and their families accounted for. Rook's van remained available, although she wanted it recorded that the cow's expression was becoming personally offensive after so many hours. Ivo said he had developed a similar view of the tower.","Noor confirmed that Mercy House was secure. Elena was disconnected, resting, and angry at the company for giving her daughter so much to do. The message made Mara laugh despite the cold air still on her face. Peta had matched more of the station identifiers to facilities where staff could be approached. The numbers alone did not tell them who occupied each station. The rescue teams would need to find people, not merely make a status light change color.","Sol showed them the handover schedule. The upper relay tier was expected to accept remote scheduling from the underground installation beneath the Municipal Water Authority compound. This would not create a supersystem able to reach every brain in Asterfall. It would give one operator coordinated access to the paired contact stations already connected to the company's facility network. The practical limits did not make the arrangement humane. They made the places that needed help specific.","Mara copied the facility references onto the map. She could not promise that every name they wanted would be in the room behind its corresponding door. She could promise to look, to keep records, and to bring people who knew how to help. That was less intoxicating than being imagined as the winner of a grand battle. It was also the work the city actually needed. She handed the map to June through the lower delivery route and kept a copy beside the console.","A new message appeared on the screen. It did not use the emergency committee's usual language. There was no heading about voluntary participation or a generous new social order. The line was short: MISS VALE, YOU HAVE INTERRUPTED A SCHEDULE.","Mara looked at the camera above the local console. It had been showing the room to someone with a clear view, not reading her mind. She faced it without attempting to hide the torn sleeve. \"It seemed badly planned,\" she said. Cassian stood beside her. The supervisor on the floor went very still.","Selene's voice came through the room speaker. \"I had hoped you would learn to distinguish influence from possession. You hold one room.\"","\"And the people in it are no longer being asked to close an occupied bridge.\"","\"An appealing scene. You have always understood how to perform for an audience.\"","Mara glanced at Sol, at Ivo's dirty hands, at Cassian's swollen wrist. The room did not look like a coronation. It looked like a repair job interrupted by a fight. \"Then you should understand why I am keeping the camera on,\" she said. Behind Selene's measured silence, another console sounded. The main relay schedule was changing again, and this time it was changing from below the greenhouse."],"questions":[{"type":"Inference / character choice","stem":"What most directly enables the guard to resist the supervisor's next order?","options":["Mara promises that joining her will remove all future consequences.","A specific warning record shows that the attempted bridge closure endangered people.","He has decided that every statement made by Mara must be correct.","The company has officially transferred all authority to the alliance."],"answer":1,"explanation":"控制台显示桥段有人仍被下令收回，给守卫一个可立足的具体事实。文本明确说这不等于他全面加入女主阵营或获得免除后果的保证。","evidence":[11],"shuffled":true}],"wordCount":1636,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0083\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">7</div><h1>11. Thirty-Eight Minutes to the Next Door","volume":"7","anchor":"chapter-0084","paragraphs":["Selene appeared on the small maintenance screen in a room full of orchids. Behind her, a glass wall showed the dim shape of a corridor leading underground. The image was calm, almost decorative. Mara had seen enough of the conservatory to know what a beautiful room could conceal. She kept her hand on the instrument case and let Cassian stand close enough that their shoulders touched.","\"You have taken a panel,\" Selene said. \"You have not taken control of the underlying operation.\"","\"You seem unusually anxious to explain the difference.\"","\"I am offering you an opportunity to avoid yet another irresponsible decision.\"","Mara looked at the supervisor seated against the wall. The man had stopped attempting to agitate the guard, who was now watching the screen with a visibly less favourable expression. \"I have seen the result of your responsible ones,\" she said. \"The bridge was an impressive arrangement.\"","Selene did not answer the accusation. She referred to the theft of proprietary materials, the forced entry into the annex, and the removal of a detained subject. Her selection was precise. It excluded the paired contact apparatus, the blocked exit, and the other people unable to leave. Mara could have spent the whole night debating the omissions. She wanted the remaining facility list more than she wanted the satisfaction of winning a quarrel on camera.","\"Who is still at the stations?\" she asked.","\"Participants.\"","\"Names.\"","\"That information is not at your disposal.\"","At the local console, Sol was preserving the report that had brought the connection to life. He did not possess a magical view into every clinical room. He could retain the paired-node identifiers and their reported states, the schedule, and the source of the command. Tessa would know how to compare those identifiers with the documents already held outside the company's system. A list was not a rescue. It was a way to begin one without guessing at every door.","Anika opened the maintenance route long enough for June, Rowan, and Tessa to reach the upper tier. They came carrying paper, a separate recording unit, and a bag of food that Rook had insisted should travel with any rescue party. The entrance made Mara's chest tighten. Their alliance had not dissolved when the first plan failed. People kept returning, bringing something the next person could use. Rowan saw her expression and held up the bag. \"I am informed that power requires carbohydrates.\"","June began recording the screen. Selene's gaze moved toward her. The company's founder had been accustomed to choosing the camera, the crop, and the narrative. Tonight she had an audience whose existence she could not adequately regulate. \"You cannot assume that every recording will survive,\" she said. June checked the independent indicator and replied, \"I seldom assume anything about equipment. It has saved me considerable embarrassment.\"","The guard received a command through his own radio. He listened, looked at the detained supervisor, and asked the sender to confirm whether the upper tier was occupied. The answer told him to secure the room. He asked again. The request would have been trivial before the moving bridge. Now its omission had acquired a human weight. He declined to close another route until the people in it were accounted for. Somewhere below, another member of the security team refused to accept responsibility for the same uncertainty.","The tower did not become theirs all at once. The main command room remained sealed. Several lower floors still answered to the company, and the public hall was full of people who wanted to go home. What changed was the confidence that an order could pass downward without resistance and acquire innocence simply because it came from above. The upper room, the old observation line, and one maintenance exit were now being held by people who had begun to ask what an instruction would actually do.","Leah reported that the families and maintenance crew were clear. Rook could move them between the tower and the theater if the street remained open. Ada Shaw's staff had unlocked rehearsal rooms for people who might need to wait somewhere warm. A conductor, disappointed at losing his festival rehearsal, was arranging chairs with unusual artistic fury. The city had found an emergency use for skills that its most expensive machinery could not imitate.","Mara allowed herself one breath of relief. Then the screen changed. The handover column disappeared, replaced by a smaller schedule with a different source marker. Anika leaned closer. The instruction had come from the underground installation, not from the company room below them. It used the relay tier but did not depend entirely on it. Selene's control had more than one physical location, and taking a tower room had not moved every switch into Mara's hand.","A shout came from the corridor. One of the lower guards had attempted to force the maintenance door before his colleague could stop him. The bar struck the window beside it. Glass fractured outward. Cassian moved in front of the people nearest the pane, catching a sharp fragment across his forearm. The cut was shallow but bled freely enough to make Mara's breath catch. The guard inside the room pushed the door shut and called down the corridor for the men to stop before they injured their own staff.","Cassian looked at the blood and then at Mara. \"An unnecessarily dramatic interruption.\" She took a clean cloth from Noor's small supply packet and pressed it against the cut. \"You are forbidden to improve this conversation with humor.\" \"A difficult restriction.\" His voice was steady; his face had lost more color than she liked. She made him sit on a low equipment case while the others secured the window. This time he obeyed before she had to ask twice.","He had come back to her from a locked room. She had seen him on the other side of glass, tired and still trying to give her a way to move. The fact that he could now sit beside her and complain did not make the fear disappear. It made the fear something she could hold in one hand while she worked with the other. He touched her wrist gently. She looked up, found him looking at her rather than the wound, and bent to kiss him.","On the screen, Selene watched. \"You know what it is to be afraid for one person,\" she said. \"Now imagine being responsible for a city.\"","Mara kept the cloth in place. \"I do not have to imagine the people behind your doors. I have met them.\"","\"Then you should understand the consequences of forcing me to complete this operation without a stable handover.\"","The schedule expanded. Several paired terminals remained active. Their contact states were reported as present. The display did not show the subjects' faces, and it did not establish their wishes. It showed the narrower, colder fact that the system was ready to address those nodes. Mara remembered a woman beneath the greenhouse with her hands against a workbench. A room could make obedience appear remarkably similar to participation when the door was locked.","Noor came over the observation channel. \"Elena is safe,\" she said. \"She is disconnected. Ewan too. We are checking the remaining facilities with Peta.\" The words steadied Mara and sharpened the danger at the same time. The people on the screen were other people's mothers, children, partners, or friends. Their unfamiliar names could not make them a lesser responsibility. She asked Noor to keep the channel open and gave Tessa room beside the copied list.","Selene sent a demand over the narrow maintenance line. Mara was to abandon the upper tier, stop the independent transmission, and surrender the copied scheduling material. In return, the company would suspend its next synchronized operation. The wording gave Selene's assurance the appearance of a contract while leaving its performance entirely in her hands. Mara read it once and passed the paper copy to June. It belonged with the rest of the record.","\"If we disconnect this room?\" Mara asked Ivo quietly.","He looked at the schedule, the plans, and the local status report. \"We can block some messages. We do not yet know what the clinical units will do when their expected signal disappears. A blank screen here is not the same as a safe person there.\"","It was the answer she needed, although it was not the answer she wanted. The final command still belonged to the underground facility. The subjects still in contact with paired nodes were the ones at risk. There would be no cloud moving across Asterfall, no impossible sweep through every childhood in the streets below. There were particular rooms, particular bodies, and a limited operation aimed at their most recent memories. That made the emergency less like a myth and more like something people might still reach in time.","Peta sent the older maintenance route beneath the conservatory. It matched the passage in Elena's notebook and the delivery drawings Rowan had already copied. The newer public entrances had changed; the heavy-equipment route remained. June laid the plan beside the clinical references. The tower had given them a voice, a record, and a view of the next move. It would not finish the work for them. Mara set her palm on the map and felt the room turn toward the same decision.","Selene's face disappeared from the screen. A final line replaced it: NEXT SYNCHRONIZED CYCLE — RECENT INTERVAL PROCESSING. Beneath it, another field warned that the associated maintenance would overwrite the local records. The operation could take from the subjects again and remove part of the trail by which it might be challenged. Mara looked at the independent recorder. Its indicator was still bright. Some copies were already beyond the company's reach. Some people were not.","The clock appeared at thirty-eight minutes.","For one instant, the room seemed to listen to it. Then a receiver clicked, somebody below asked for a safe exit, and Noor began reading a facility address aloud. The city had not stopped. Neither would they. Mara took another clean strip for Cassian's arm and reached for the plan. Selene had offered a choice between surrender and harm. Mara intended to find the doors that the choice had carefully left unmentioned."],"questions":[{"type":"Inference / scope of control","stem":"Why is taking the upper tower room insufficient to secure the remaining subjects immediately?","options":["The final command remains at the underground facility, and the local units' response to a lost signal is not yet safely known.","Disconnecting the tower has already been proved to stop every procedure harmlessly.","The team has no record of any active terminal.","The apparatus can reach every citizen without any physical connection."],"answer":0,"explanation":"控制范围有限：最终指令源在地下设施，本地设备失去信号后会如何动作尚未核实。不能把控制一间塔房等同于控制全部设备，也不能扩成无接触全城抹记忆。","evidence":[28,29],"shuffled":true}],"wordCount":1707,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0084\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">7</div><h1>12.
